54	\paragraph{Margin stratification.}
55	To assess the robustness of individual $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, we stratify the seven continuation-mode configurations by their margin above $\theta$ (CI lower $-$ $\theta$; Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}).
56	Three configurations across three families show confident margins exceeding 1\,pp: Pythia-1.4B (4.5\,pp), Qwen-base (1.7\,pp), and Qwen-14B (1.2\,pp).
57	Four have marginal support (${<}\,1$\,pp): Qwen-7B (0.8\,pp), OLMo-1B (0.5\,pp), LLaMA-8B (0.3\,pp), and Qwen-cont (0.2\,pp); the last drops to $\Kempirical{=}1$ under cross-scorer control (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
58	We emphasize that $\Kempirical$ is determined by bootstrap CI (Eq.~\ref{eq:kstar}), not by permutation $p$-values, which serve as supplementary validation.
59	The $\theta$-invariant domain ordering (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}) is supported by the confident configurations alone and does not depend on marginal cases.
60	
61	%% ====================================================================
62	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer and Its Limits}
63	\label{sec:transfer}
64	
65	\begin{figure}[t]
66	\centering
67	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf}
68	\caption{Cross-model transfer heatmap. Each cell shows 1v2 boundary $\auc$ when trained on the row model and tested on the column model. Diagonal entries (bold) are in-domain; off-diagonal entries show transfer performance. Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo transfer retains $\auc > \theta$ (0.590--0.599); Qwen-rewrite transfers above $\theta$ to Qwen-cont (0.619) but not to Pythia/OLMo.}
69	\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}
70	\end{figure}
71	
72	Aggregate transfer is strong: continuation-mode classifiers retain 80--92\% ordinal accuracy across generators at $\leq$1.5B (Table~\ref{tab:s1_transfer}), and 96--100\% mean pairwise AUC at 7B (Appendix~\ref{sec:7b_transfer}).
73	However, boundary-level transfer is fragile: at 1.5B, most off-diagonal 1v2 $\auc$ values fall below $\theta$ (Figure~\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap}); at 7B, 4/6 off-diagonal 1v2 pairs likewise fall below $\theta$, so $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments do not transfer at the boundary even as overall discriminability improves with scale.
74	Qwen-rewrite classifiers transfer poorly (61--63\%), consistent with reliance on mode-specific artifacts.
75	
76	\paragraph{Cross-scorer validation.}
77	Replacing the self-scorer with Pythia-1.4B or OLMo-1B preserves feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$; Table~\ref{tab:crossscorer}) and $\Kempirical$ in 20/22 cross-scorer configurations on C4 and arXiv; the two exceptions are the most marginal boundary configurations (AUC within 0.002 of $\theta$).
78	Notably, the most marginal self-scored configuration (Qwen-cont, CI lower $= 0.602$, margin $0.2$\,pp) drops to $\Kempirical{=}1$ under Pythia and OLMo scoring (1v2 AUC $= 0.592$/$0.586$), reducing the continuation-mode count from 7/9 to 6/9 under strict cross-scorer control.
79	For arXiv, Pythia-1.4B cross-scoring preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ for 3/4 generators (2v3 $\auc$: 0.608--0.632); Qwen-7B drops to borderline $\Kempirical{=}2$ ($\Delta = -2.7$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}).
80	A three-way ANOVA confirms Depth-Pair dominates variance ($\eta^2_p = 0.998$; Scorer: $\eta^2_p = 0.192$; Table~\ref{tab:anova_crossscorer}); replacing the 1.5B scorer with Qwen2.5-7B preserves $\Kempirical$ identically ($\Delta\,\auc < 1$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:scorer_scale}).
81	
82	%% ====================================================================
83	\subsection{Feature Ordering and Generation Mode}
84	\label{sec:feature_degradation}
85	\label{sec:generation_mode}
86	
87	Among continuation-mode models, single-feature $\auc$ rankings are highly correlated ($\rho \geq 0.85$; Table~\ref{tab:top_features}), with the top-3 (\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}, \texttt{surp\_std}, \texttt{surp\_d2\_std}) shared across families; no single subset of size ${<}8$ sustains $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_subset}), and surprisal dominates on arXiv while C4 relies on feature combinations (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_ablation}).
88	Qwen-rewrite diverges ($\Delta = +8.4$\,pp), consistent with mode-specific artifacts; continuation mode is the canonical condition.
89	
90	%% ====================================================================
91	\subsection{Neural Probe}

92	\label{sec:neural_probe}
93	
94	A neural MLP probe on frozen encoder states (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}) raises $K^*_{\text{neural}}$ to 3 for Qwen (2v3 $\auc = 0.616$) but not Pythia or OLMo ($\auc_{2v3}$: 0.596, 0.589), reflecting self-familiarity artifacts; the 15D statistical features yield consistent $\Kempirical = 2$ across all families.
95	
96	\paragraph{Fine-tuned DeBERTa upper bound.}
97	DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifiers raise 1v2 $\auc$ over LightGBM at all three scales (1.5B: $0.705$ vs.\ $0.626$; 14B: $0.678$ vs.\ $0.621$; 32B: $0.694$ vs.\ $0.618$) yet preserve $\Kempirical{=}2$ (all 2v3 CI lower ${<}\,\theta$; Table~\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}); LightGBM shifts by ${<}\,0.5$\,pp across 1.5B--32B.
98	The classifier gap reveals a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure: zero on C4 and Code (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}2$, matching OBD), but $+2$ on Wikipedia (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}3$; OLMo excluded) and $+1$ on arXiv (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}4$, 3v4 $\auc = 0.624$; Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}); cross-transfer on Wikipedia mirrors the OBD pattern ($\Kempirical{=}3$ drops to ${\sim}2$; Table~\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_transfer}).
99	The C4 result ($\Kempirical_{\text{DeBERTa}} = \Kempirical_{\text{OBD}} = 2$) establishes a representation-independent boundary: even a 184M-parameter fine-tuned encoder cannot surpass $\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text.
100	The neural probe (Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}), which uses frozen encoder representations with a linear head, provides an intermediate reference point between 15D statistics and full DeBERTa fine-tuning.
101	
102	\noindent\begin{minipage}{\columnwidth}
103	\centering
104	\footnotesize
105	\captionof{table}{DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\ LightGBM on C4 generations at three scales. Both classifiers yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at 1.5B, 14B, and 32B.}
106	\label{tab:deberta_comparison}
107	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
108	\toprule
109	& \textbf{0v1} & \textbf{1v2} & \textbf{2v3} & \textbf{3v4} & \textbf{4v5} \\
110	\midrule
111	LightGBM (1.5B) & ---  & .626 & .549 & ---  & --- \\
112	DeBERTa (1.5B)  & .992 & .705 & .599 & ---  & --- \\
113	\midrule
114	LightGBM (14B)  & .960 & .621 & .549 & .524 & --- \\
115	DeBERTa (14B)   & .994 & .678 & .590 & .565 & --- \\
116	\midrule

123	%% ====================================================================
124	\subsection{Cross-Domain Variation}
125	\label{sec:cross_domain}
126	
127	To test whether $\Kempirical$ reflects domain structure or model identity, we extend evaluation to arXiv ($n{=}2{,}200$), Wikipedia ($n{=}5{,}000$), and Code ($n{=}5{,}000$) across six configurations spanning four families (Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}).
128	
129	\paragraph{arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$): five-model cross-validation.}
130	All five domain-competent models yield $\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\auc$: 0.6215--0.6545, all ${>}\,\theta$), reflecting formulaic features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns) that degrade slowly and supply persistent depth signal across four families (1.4B--8B).
131	Removing LaTeX markup reduces 2v3 AUC by ${\sim}$1.8\,pp but preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ (CI lower $= 0.610$; Table~\ref{tab:latex_ablation}).
132	OLMo-1B is a capacity failure (0v1 JSD $= 0.33$), not a counter-example; Mistral-7B provides the strongest domain-dependence evidence ($\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4 but $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv).
133	The arXiv subset ($n{=}2{,}200$ per depth) is smaller than C4 ($n{=}5{,}000$); at equal sample size, wider bootstrap CIs shift borderline C4 configurations below $\theta$, yielding $\Kempirical{=}1$; the $\Kempirical{=}2$ verdict for these marginal C4 cases requires ${\geq}5{,}000$ samples.
134	
135	\paragraph{Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$): single-generation saturation.}
136	With cross-family scorer control, all six models yield $\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\auc$: 0.498--0.595, all below $\theta$): encyclopedic regularity provides minimal signal beyond the 0v1 boundary.
137	Same-family scoring inflates Qwen to $\Kempirical{=}2$ ($\Delta = +2.4$\,pp); cross-family scoring controls this bias (Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}).
138	
139	\paragraph{Code ($\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$): cross-modality confirmation.}
140	Python functions from CodeSearchNet~\cite{husain2019codesearchnet} ($n{=}5{,}000$) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ for four models (1v2 $\auc$: 0.615--0.739; Table~\ref{tab:code_crossmodel_kstar}) with the same sharp 2v3 collapse as C4 (JSD ratio $11.7$--$13.5\times$); Pythia-1.4B is capacity-limited ($\Kempirical{=}1$, $\auc = 0.598$; cross-family scorer: $\Delta \leq 1.9$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:code_domain}).
141

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The PI (Director) of gen-depth-contamination project is pushing a paper from Overall 3.5 to 4.0 for EMNLP 2026 ARR (deadline May 25, 2026). Co-author 冯元宁 is systematically auditing the paper for claim-data consistency issues and formatting problems. User 游承松 is also involved. The Director manages the Agent team, processes Reviewer findings, and directly edits the paper LaTeX. Key goals this session: (a) handle data integrity crisis (OLMo/Pythia original data lost), (b) fix consistency issues found by 冯元宁, (c) prepare for paper claim updates once rerun experiments complete.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **K* (Discrimination Boundary)**: Maximum generational depth where adjacent generations are statistically distinguishable. Defined by Eq.1: CI lower bound ≥ θ.
   - **θ=0.60**: Default threshold for K* determination
   - **Domain ordering**: arXiv(K*=3) > C4(K*=2) = Code(K*=2) > Wikipedia(K*=1) under 15D features
   - **OLMo K*=1 correction (D096)**: OLMo-1B's original K*=2 (AUC=0.6145) unreproducible; standardized pipeline rerun gives AUC=0.508, K*=1
   - **Scenario planning**: If Pythia-1.4B also drops to K*=1, "four of six families K*=2" becomes "two of six" — requires narrative restructuring to "K*≤2 universal ceiling"
   - **EMNLP 2026 page limits**: Strict 8-page main text, unlimited references/appendix, Limitations/Ethics don't count
   - **LaTeX float management**: minipage vs float table for column balancing

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **appendix_extended.tex**
     - Added D.1 Cross-Scorer Validation subsection to organize orphaned tables
     - Change: section intro paragraph removed, `\subsection{Cross-Scorer Validation}` and `\label{sec:cross_scorer_validation}` added before cross-scorer tables (lines 1-36 restructured)
     ```latex
     \section{Extended Analysis}

     \begin{table}[htbp]

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\auc_{0v1} \geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\theta$).
19	
20	\paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}
21	$\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves the question of filtering granularity: graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}), and the $\theta$-invariant domain ordering extends this across domains (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}; rate sweeps in Appendix~\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).
22	
23	\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain.}
24	All main results use prompt-chain ($\Kempirical{=}2$, text-intrinsic information loss).
25	Retrain-chain yields $\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:retrain_chain}), bounding the range; whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.
26	
27	\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}
28	Token-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).
29	Domain determines the $\Kempirical$ ordering; Wikipedia's template-driven structure causes single-generation saturation ($\Kempirical{=}1$; Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).
30	
31	\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\Kempirical_{\text{15D}}$ vs.\ $\Kempirical_{\text{intrinsic}}$.}
32	Absolute $\Kempirical$ values are \emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv (Tables~\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_comparison}, \ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), but matches the 15D bound on C4 and Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \emph{representation-independent boundary} on web text (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}).
33	The progression 15D $\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\text{neural}}{=}3$) $\to$ DeBERTa ($\Kempirical{=}4$) shows diminishing returns, suggesting $K^*_\text{theory} \approx 3$--$4$; permutation importance confirms saturation at 2v3 (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_importance}).
34	
35	\paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}
36	Multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains, partially addressing the 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}): domain-calibrated ceilings hold under mixed-generator training even though $\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed when the generator is unknown.
37	
38

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	We present the first empirical measurement of $\Kempirical$ across six architecture families, four domains, and four decoding strategies.
5	For web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ holds across four of six families: a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper recursive content.
6	The $\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across generators under 15D features; C4/Code $\Kempirical{=}2$ is representation-independent (confirmed by DeBERTa), while 15D provides a conservative lower bound on structured domains.
7	Leave-one-family-out cross-validation confirms that $\theta{=}0.60$ is robust across held-out families, and a non-Qwen derivation with Qwen validation rules out scorer overfitting.
8	For data curation, binary filtering at the $\Kempirical$ boundary suffices ($\Delta \leq 0.53$\,pp for graduated vs.\ no filtering); retrain-chain experiments extend the range to $\Kempirical \in [2, 7]$, with scaling beyond 14B and mixed chains remaining open.
9	
```

36	Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}(a) show $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for 7/9 continuation-mode C4 configurations across four of six tested architecture families (4 Qwen + 3 non-Qwen; 1v2 $\auc$ range: 0.6112--0.6539; all CI lower bounds $> \theta$; permutation $p \leq 10^{-4}$).
37	The 1v2 vs.\ 2v3 AUC gap ranges from 5.0 to 10.6\,pp across configurations, exhibiting a sharp cliff at the boundary: all 2v3 values fall below $\theta$ while 1v2 values remain 1--10\,pp above it.
38	Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B are competence-limited at $\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\auc$: 0.598, CI $[0.588, 0.608]$; and 0.594, respectively): both achieve near-perfect 0v1 separation but fail to sustain 1v2 above $\theta$.
39	Among the remaining non-Qwen families, Pythia and OLMo yield clear $\Kempirical{=}2$; LLaMA-3.1-8B is borderline (1v2 $\auc = 0.6123$, CI lower $= 0.603$).
40	An independent F1@0.50 analysis confirms the same boundary for all configurations (Appendix~\ref{sec:f1_validation}).
41	All four decoding strategies yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Table~\ref{tab:s2_decoding}), and $\Kempirical{=}2$ persists from 1.5B through 14B (Appendix~\ref{sec:boundary_extended}).
42	
43	\begin{figure*}[t]
44	\centering
45	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig_kstar_theta.pdf}
46	\caption{(a) Pairwise $\auc$ decay across depth pairs for model-mode configurations (solid) and decoding strategies (dashed); horizontal dashed line marks $\theta = 0.60$; AUC computed on held-out folds. (b) $\Kempirical(\theta)$ sensitivity: 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B yield $\Kempirical{=}1$); at $\theta{=}0.70$, all drop to $\Kempirical{=}1$.}
47	\label{fig:kstar_theta}
48	\end{figure*}
49	
50	Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta} shows $\Kempirical$ as a function of $\theta$: web text $\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$, with transitions to $\Kempirical{=}3$ at $\theta{=}0.55$ (Qwen-rewrite) and $\Kempirical{=}1$ at $\theta{=}0.70$; the domain ordering is preserved across the full range (Table~\ref{tab:theta_sensitivity}).
51	A changepoint analysis (PELT~\cite{killick2012optimal}) provides complementary, threshold-free evidence: exact agreement is 18/37 (49\%; linear weighted $\kappa{=}0.42$, moderate agreement), rising to 35/37 (95\%) within $\pm 1$; PELT is systematically conservative ($\Kempirical_{\text{PELT}} \leq \Kempirical_{\text{OBD}}$ in all 19 disagreements; Appendix~\ref{sec:changepoint_validation}).
52	We do not rely on changepoint agreement for $\Kempirical$ determination.
53	
54	\paragraph{Margin stratification.}
55	To assess the robustness of individual $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, we stratify the seven continuation-mode configurations by their margin above $\theta$ (CI lower $-$ $\theta$; Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}).
56	Three configurations across three families show confident margins exceeding 1\,pp: Pythia-1.4B (4.5\,pp), Qwen-base (1.7\,pp), and Qwen-14B (1.2\,pp).
57	Four have marginal support (${<}\,1$\,pp): Qwen-7B (0.8\,pp), OLMo-1B (0.5\,pp), LLaMA-8B (0.3\,pp), and Qwen-cont (0.2\,pp); the last drops to $\Kempirical{=}1$ under cross-scorer control (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
58	We emphasize that $\Kempirical$ is determined by bootstrap CI (Eq.~\ref{eq:kstar}), not by permutation $p$-values, which serve as supplementary validation.
59	The $\theta$-invariant domain ordering (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}) is supported by the confident configurations alone and does not depend on marginal cases.
60	
61	%% ====================================================================
62	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer and Its Limits}
63	\label{sec:transfer}
64	
65	\begin{figure}[t]
66	\centering
67	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf}
68	\caption{Cross-model transfer heatmap. Each cell shows 1v2 boundary $\auc$ when trained on the row model and tested on the column model. Diagonal entries (bold) are in-domain; off-diagonal entries show transfer performance. Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo transfer retains $\auc > \theta$ (0.590--0.599); Qwen-rewrite transfers above $\theta$ to Qwen-cont (0.619) but not to Pythia/OLMo.}
69	\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}
70	\end{figure}
71	
72	Aggregate transfer is strong: continuation-mode classifiers retain 80--92\% ordinal accuracy across generators at $\leq$1.5B (Table~\ref{tab:s1_transfer}), and 96--100\% mean pairwise AUC at 7B (Appendix~\ref{sec:7b_transfer}).
73	However, boundary-level transfer is fragile: at 1.5B, most off-diagonal 1v2 $\auc$ values fall below $\theta$ (Figure~\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap}); at 7B, 4/6 off-diagonal 1v2 pairs likewise fall below $\theta$, so $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments do not transfer at the boundary even as overall discriminability improves with scale.
74	Qwen-rewrite classifiers transfer poorly (61--63\%), consistent with reliance on mode-specific artifacts.
75	
76	\paragraph{Cross-scorer validation.}
77	Replacing the self-scorer with Pythia-1.4B or OLMo-1B preserves feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$; Table~\ref{tab:crossscorer}) and $\Kempirical$ in 20/22 cross-scorer configurations on C4 and arXiv; the two exceptions are the most marginal boundary configurations (AUC within 0.002 of $\theta$).
78	Notably, the most marginal self-scored configuration (Qwen-cont, CI lower $= 0.602$, margin $0.2$\,pp) drops to $\Kempirical{=}1$ under Pythia and OLMo scoring (1v2 AUC $= 0.592$/$0.586$), reducing the continuation-mode count from 7/9 to 6/9 under strict cross-scorer control.
79	For arXiv, Pythia-1.4B cross-scoring preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ for 3/4 generators (2v3 $\auc$: 0.608--0.632); Qwen-7B drops to borderline $\Kempirical{=}2$ ($\Delta = -2.7$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}).
80	A three-way ANOVA confirms Depth-Pair dominates variance ($\eta^2_p = 0.998$; Scorer: $\eta^2_p = 0.192$; Table~\ref{tab:anova_crossscorer}); replacing the 1.5B scorer with Qwen2.5-7B preserves $\Kempirical$ identically ($\Delta\,\auc < 1$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:scorer_scale}).
81	
82	%% ====================================================================
83	\subsection{Feature Ordering and Generation Mode}
84	\label{sec:feature_degradation}
85	\label{sec:generation_mode}
86	
87	Among continuation-mode models, single-feature $\auc$ rankings are highly correlated ($\rho \geq 0.85$; Table~\ref{tab:top_features}), with the top-3 (\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}, \texttt{surp\_std}, \texttt{surp\_d2\_std}) shared across families; no single subset of size ${<}8$ sustains $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_subset}), and surprisal dominates on arXiv while C4 relies on feature combinations (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_ablation}).
88	Qwen-rewrite diverges ($\Delta = +8.4$\,pp), consistent with mode-specific artifacts; continuation mode is the canonical condition.
89	
90	%% ====================================================================
91	\subsection{Neural Probe}
92	\label{sec:neural_probe}
93	
94	A neural MLP probe on frozen encoder states (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}) raises $K^*_{\text{neural}}$ to 3 for Qwen (2v3 $\auc = 0.616$) but not Pythia or OLMo ($\auc_{2v3}$: 0.596, 0.589), reflecting self-familiarity artifacts; the 15D statistical features yield consistent $\Kempirical = 2$ across all families.
95	
96	\paragraph{Fine-tuned DeBERTa upper bound.}
97	DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifiers raise 1v2 $\auc$ over LightGBM at all three scales (1.5B: $0.705$ vs.\ $0.626$; 14B: $0.678$ vs.\ $0.621$; 32B: $0.694$ vs.\ $0.618$) yet preserve $\Kempirical{=}2$ (all 2v3 CI lower ${<}\,\theta$; Table~\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}); LightGBM shifts by ${<}\,0.5$\,pp across 1.5B--32B.
98	The classifier gap reveals a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure: zero on C4 and Code (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}2$, matching OBD), but $+2$ on Wikipedia (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}3$; OLMo excluded) and $+1$ on arXiv (DeBERTa $\Kempirical{=}4$, 3v4 $\auc = 0.624$; Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}); cross-transfer on Wikipedia mirrors the OBD pattern ($\Kempirical{=}3$ drops to ${\sim}2$; Table~\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_transfer}).
99	The C4 result ($\Kempirical_{\text{DeBERTa}} = \Kempirical_{\text{OBD}} = 2$) establishes a representation-independent boundary: even a 184M-parameter fine-tuned encoder cannot surpass $\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text.
100	The neural probe (Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}), which uses frozen encoder representations with a linear head, provides an intermediate reference point between 15D statistics and full DeBERTa fine-tuning.
101

122	
123	%% ====================================================================
124	\subsection{Cross-Domain Variation}
125	\label{sec:cross_domain}
126	
127	To test whether $\Kempirical$ reflects domain structure or model identity, we extend evaluation to arXiv ($n{=}2{,}200$), Wikipedia ($n{=}5{,}000$), and Code ($n{=}5{,}000$) across six configurations spanning four families (Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}).
128	
129	\paragraph{arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$): five-model cross-validation.}
130	All five domain-competent models yield $\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\auc$: 0.6215--0.6545, all ${>}\,\theta$), reflecting formulaic features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns) that degrade slowly and supply persistent depth signal across four families (1.4B--8B).
131	Removing LaTeX markup reduces 2v3 AUC by ${\sim}$1.8\,pp but preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ (CI lower $= 0.610$; Table~\ref{tab:latex_ablation}).
132	OLMo-1B is a capacity failure (0v1 JSD $= 0.33$), not a counter-example; Mistral-7B provides the strongest domain-dependence evidence ($\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4 but $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv).
133	The arXiv subset ($n{=}2{,}200$ per depth) is smaller than C4 ($n{=}5{,}000$); at equal sample size, wider bootstrap CIs shift borderline C4 configurations below $\theta$, yielding $\Kempirical{=}1$; the $\Kempirical{=}2$ verdict for these marginal C4 cases requires ${\geq}5{,}000$ samples.
134	
135	\paragraph{Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$): single-generation saturation.}
136	With cross-family scorer control, all six models yield $\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\auc$: 0.498--0.595, all below $\theta$): encyclopedic regularity provides minimal signal beyond the 0v1 boundary.
137	Same-family scoring inflates Qwen to $\Kempirical{=}2$ ($\Delta = +2.4$\,pp); cross-family scoring controls this bias (Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}).
138	
139	\paragraph{Code ($\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$): cross-modality confirmation.}
140	Python functions from CodeSearchNet~\cite{husain2019codesearchnet} ($n{=}5{,}000$) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ for four models (1v2 $\auc$: 0.615--0.739; Table~\ref{tab:code_crossmodel_kstar}) with the same sharp 2v3 collapse as C4 (JSD ratio $11.7$--$13.5\times$); Pythia-1.4B is capacity-limited ($\Kempirical{=}1$, $\auc = 0.598$; cross-family scorer: $\Delta \leq 1.9$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:code_domain}).
141	
142	\paragraph{Multi-source generator mixing.}
143	Multi-source pooling (three generators per domain; Appendix~\ref{sec:multi_source_details}) preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains (C4 $\Kempirical{=}2$, unified 1v2 $\auc = 0.711$, generator identity $+0.010$ mean $\auc$ [range: $+0.001$, $+0.022$]; $\Kempirical$ unchanged in all eight configurations; arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$; Code $\Kempirical{=}2$; Wikipedia $\Kempirical{=}1$), independently confirming the cross-scorer correction on Wikipedia.
144

Wikipedia values use cross-family scorer (Pythia-1.4B). $^*$OLMo arXiv: capacity failure. $^\dagger$Same-family scorer yields $\Kempirical{=}2$; controlled value shown. $^\ddagger$Borderline: Pythia CI upper $= 0.603$, Mistral CI lower $= 0.603$.}
149	\label{tab:domain_kstar}

166	\paragraph{Summary.}
167	\label{sec:binary_confidence}
168	$\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for web text holds across four of six families (1B--14B), four decoding strategies, and multi-source pools (Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B: $\Kempirical{=}1$; binary detectors: $K^*_{\text{binary}} \leq 1$, Table~\ref{tab:s9_binary_spearman}).
169	The domain ordering arXiv $>$ C4 $=$ Code $>$ Wikipedia is $\theta$-invariant; scale and architecture do not alter this ordering, confirming structural complexity as the primary determinant.

7B/14B use Qwen-1.5B scorer ($^\\ddagger$).\nExtended results in Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}.}\n\\label{tab:kstar_ci}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccccccc}\n\\toprule\n & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{Qwen-rw\\rlap{$^\\dagger$}} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{Pythia} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{OLMo} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{Qwen-cont} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{Qwen-7B\\rlap{$^\\ddagger$}} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-3}\\cmidrule(lr){4-5}\\cmidrule(lr){6-7}\\cmidrule(lr){8-9}\\cmidrule(lr){10-11}\n & $\\auc$ & 95\\% CI & $\\auc$ & 95\\% CI & $\\auc$ & 95\\% CI & $\\auc$ & 95\\% CI & $\\auc$ & 95\\% CI \\\\\n\\midrule\n0v1 & .9956 & & .9932 & & .9970 & & .9971 & & .9638 & [.961,\\,.967] \\\\\n1v2 & .6955 & [.686,\\,.704]\\rlap{$^*$} & .6539 & [.645,\\,.663]\\rlap{$^*$} & .6145 & [.605,\\,.625]\\rlap{$^*$} & .6112 & [.602,\\,.621]\\rlap{$^*$} & .6168 & [.608,\\,.626]\\rlap{$^*$} \\\\\n2v3 & .5893 & [.580,\\,.599] & .5514 & [.542,\\,.563] & .5496 & [.540,\\,.560] & .5609 & [.551,\\,.571] & .5492 & [.539,\\,.559] \\\\\n3v4 & .5435 & & .5152 & & .5091 & & .5409 & & .5276 & \\\\\n4v5 & .5340 & & .5136 & & .5045 & & .5141 & & .5085 & \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\multicolumn{11}{l}{\\footnotesize $^*$Permutation $p \\leq 10^{-4}$ for all 1v2 comparisons shown (BH-corrected $q_{\\max} = 0.003$).} \\\\\n\\multicolumn{11}{l}{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$Rewrite mode (ablation); excluded from continuation-mode $\\Kempirical$ claim.} \\\\\n\\multicolumn{11}{l}{\\footnotesize $^\\ddagger$Generator: Qwen2.5-7B (fp16); scorer: Qwen2.5-1.5B.}\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci} and Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}(a) show $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ for 7/9 continuation-mode C4 configurations across four of six tested architecture families (4 Qwen + 3 non-Qwen; 1v2 $\\auc$ range: 0.6112--0.6539; all CI lower bounds $> \\theta$; permutation $p \\leq 10^{-4}$).\nThe 1v2 vs.\\ 2v3 AUC gap ranges from 5.0 to 10.6\\,pp across configurations, exhibiting a sharp cliff at the boundary: all 2v3 values fall below $\\theta$ while 1v2 values remain 1--10\\,pp above it.\nGemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B are competence-limited at $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\\auc$: 0.598, CI $[0.588, 0.608]$; and 0.594, respectively): both achieve near-perfect 0v1 separation but fail to sustain 1v2 above $\\theta$.\nAmong the remaining non-Qwen families, Pythia and OLMo yield clear $\\Kempirical{=}2$; LLaMA-3.1-8B is borderline (1v2 $\\auc = 0.6123$, CI lower $= 0.603$).\nAn independent F1@0.50 analysis confirms the same boundary for all configurations (Appendix~\\ref{sec:f1_validation}).\nAll four decoding strategies yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Table~\\ref{tab:s2_decoding}), and $\\Kempirical{=}2$ persists from 1.5B through 14B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:boundary_extended}).\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/fig_kstar_theta.pdf}\n\\caption{(a) Pairwise $\\auc$ decay across depth pairs for model-mode configurations (solid) and decoding strategies (dashed); horizontal dashed line marks $\\theta = 0.60$; AUC computed on held-out folds. (b) $\\Kempirical(\\theta)$ sensitivity: 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$); at $\\theta{=}0.70$, all drop to $\\Kempirical{=}1$.}\n\\label{fig:kstar_theta}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nFigure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} shows $\\Kempirical$ as a function of $\\theta$: web text $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.625]$, with transitions to $\\Kempirical{=}3$ at $\\theta{=}0.55$ (Qwen-rewrite) and $\\Kempirical{=}1$ at $\\theta{=}0.70$; the domain ordering is preserved across the full range (Table~\\ref{tab:theta_sensitivity}).\nA changepoint analysis (PELT~\\cite{killick2012optimal}) provides complementary, threshold-free evidence: exact agreement is 18/37 (49\\%; linear weighted $\\kappa{=}0.42$, moderate agreement), rising to 35/37 (95\\%) within $\\pm 1$; PELT is systematically conservative ($\\Kempirical_{\\text{PELT}} \\leq \\Kempirical_{\\text{OBD}}$ in all 19 disagreements; Appendix~\\ref{sec:changepoint_validation}).\nWe do not rely on changepoint agreement for $\\Kempirical$ determination.\n\n\\paragraph{Margin stratification.}\nTo assess the robustness of individual $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, we stratify the seven continuation-mode configurations by their margin above $\\theta$ (CI lower $-$ $\\theta$; Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}).\nThree configurations across three families show confident margins exceeding 1\\,pp: Pythia-1.4B (4.5\\,pp), Qwen-base (1.7\\,pp), and Qwen-14B (1.2\\,pp).\nFour have marginal support (${<}\\,1$\\,pp): Qwen-7B (0.8\\,pp), OLMo-1B (0.5\\,pp), LLaMA-8B (0.3\\,pp), and Qwen-cont (0.2\\,pp); the last drops to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ under cross-scorer control (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\nWe emphasize that $\\Kempirical$ is determined by bootstrap CI (Eq.~\\ref{eq:kstar}), not by permutation $p$-values, which serve as supplementary validation.\nThe $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}) is supported by the confident configurations alone and does not depend on marginal cases.\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer and Its Limits}\n\\label{sec:transfer}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-model transfer heatmap. Each cell shows 1v2 boundary $\\auc$ when trained on the row model and tested on the column model. Diagonal entries (bold) are in-domain; off-diagonal entries show transfer performance. Pythia$\\leftrightarrow$OLMo transfer retains $\\auc > \\theta$ (0.590--0.599); Qwen-rewrite transfers above $\\theta$ to Qwen-cont (0.619) but not to Pythia/OLMo.}\n\\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\nAggregate transfer is strong: continuation-mode classifiers retain 80--92\\% ordinal accuracy across generators at $\\leq$1.5B (Table~\\ref{tab:s1_transfer}), and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC at 7B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}).\nHowever, boundary-level transfer is fragile: at 1.5B, most off-diagonal 1v2 $\\auc$ values fall below $\\theta$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap}); at 7B, 4/6 off-diagonal 1v2 pairs likewise fall below $\\theta$, so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments do not transfer at the boundary even as overall discriminability improves with scale.\nQwen-rewrite classifiers transfer poorly (61--63\\%), consistent with reliance on mode-specific artifacts.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-scorer validation.}\nReplacing the self-scorer with Pythia-1.4B or OLMo-1B preserves feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$; Table~\\ref{tab:crossscorer}) and $\\Kempirical$ in 20/22 cross-scorer configurations on C4 and arXiv; the two exceptions are the most marginal boundary configurations (AUC within 0.002 of $\\theta$).\nNotably, the most marginal self-scored configuration (Qwen-cont, CI lower $= 0.602$, margin $0.2$\\,pp) drops to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ under Pythia and OLMo scoring (1v2 AUC $= 0.592$/$0.586$), reducing the continuation-mode count from 7/9 to 6/9 under strict cross-scorer control.\nFor arXiv, Pythia-1.4B cross-scoring preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for 3/4 generators (2v3 $\\auc$: 0.608--0.632); Qwen-7B drops to borderline $\\Kempirical{=}2$ ($\\Delta = -2.7$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}).\nA three-way ANOVA confirms Depth-Pair dominates variance ($\\eta^2_p = 0.998$; Scorer: $\\eta^2_p = 0.192$; Table~\\ref{tab:anova_crossscorer}); replacing the 1.5B scorer with Qwen2.5-7B preserves $\\Kempirical$ identically ($\\Delta\\,\\auc < 1$\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{sec:scorer_scale}).\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Feature Ordering and Generation Mode}\n\\label{sec:feature_degradation}\n\\label{sec:generation_mode}\n\nAmong continuation-mode models, single-feature $\\auc$ rankings are highly correlated ($\\rho \\geq 0.85$; Table~\\ref{tab:top_features}), with the top-3 (\\texttt{surp\\_d1\\_std}, \\texttt{surp\\_std}, \\texttt{surp\\_d2\\_std}) shared across families; no single subset of size ${<}8$ sustains $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:feature_subset}), and surprisal dominates on arXiv while C4 relies on feature combinations (Appendix~\\ref{sec:feature_ablation}).\nQwen-rewrite diverges ($\\Delta = +8.4$\\,pp), consistent with mode-specific artifacts; continuation mode is the canonical condition.\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Neural Probe}\n\\label{sec:neural_probe}\n\nA neural MLP probe on frozen encoder states (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}) raises $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ to 3 for Qwen (2v3 $\\auc = 0.616$) but not Pythia or OLMo ($\\auc_{2v3}$: 0.596, 0.589), reflecting self-familiarity artifacts; the 15D statistical features yield consistent $\\Kempirical = 2$ across all families.\n\n\\paragraph{Fine-tuned DeBERTa upper bound.}\nDeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifiers raise 1v2 $\\auc$ over LightGBM at all three scales (1.5B: $0.705$ vs.\\ $0.626$; 14B: $0.678$ vs.\\ $0.621$; 32B: $0.694$ vs.\\ $0.618$) yet preserve $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (all 2v3 CI lower ${<}\\,\\theta$; Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}); LightGBM shifts by ${<}\\,0.5$\\,pp across 1.5B--32B.\nThe classifier gap reveals a feature-set-dependent two-tier structure: zero on C4 and Code (DeBERTa $\\Kempirical{=}2$, matching OBD), but $+2$ on Wikipedia (DeBERTa $\\Kempirical{=}3$; OLMo excluded) and $+1$ on arXiv (DeBERTa $\\Kempirical{=}4$, 3v4 $\\auc = 0.624$; Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}); cross-transfer on Wikipedia mirrors the OBD pattern ($\\Kempirical{=}3$ drops to ${\\sim}2$; Table~\\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_transfer}).\nThe C4 result ($\\Kempirical_{\\text{DeBERTa}} = \\Kempirical_{\\text{OBD}} = 2$) establishes a representation-independent boundary: even a 184M-parameter fine-tuned encoder cannot surpass $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text.\nThe neural probe (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}), which uses frozen encoder representations with a linear head, provides an intermediate reference point between 15D statistics and full DeBERTa fine-tuning.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\caption{DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\\ LightGBM on C4 generations at three scales. Both classifiers yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at 1.5B, 14B, and 32B.}\n\\label{tab:deberta_comparison}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n& \\textbf{0v1} & \\textbf{1v2} & \\textbf{2v3} & \\textbf{3v4} & \\textbf{4v5} \\\\\n\\midrule\nLightGBM (1.5B) & ---  & .626 & .549 & ---  & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa (1.5B)  & .992 & .705 & .599 & ---  & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nLightGBM (14B)  & .960 & .621 & .549 & .524 & --- \\\\\nDeBERTa (14B)   & .994 & .678 & .590 & .565 & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\nLightGBM (32B)  & .958 & .618 & .545 & .522 & .516 \\\\\nDeBERTa (32B)   & .993 & .694 & .594 & .567 & .543 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n%% ====================================================================\n\\subsection{Cross-Domain Variation}\n\\label{sec:cross_domain}\n\nTo test whether $\\Kempirical$ reflects domain structure or model identity, we extend evaluation to arXiv ($n{=}2{,}200$), Wikipedia ($n{=}5{,}000$), and Code ($n{=}5{,}000$) across six configurations spanning four families (Table~\\ref{tab:domain_kstar}).\n\n\\paragraph{arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$): five-model cross-validation.}\nAll five domain-competent models yield $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\\auc$: 0.6215--0.6545, all ${>}\\,\\theta$), reflecting formulaic features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns) that degrade slowly and supply persistent depth signal across four families (1.4B--8B).\nRemoving LaTeX markup reduces 2v3 AUC by ${\\sim}$1.8\\,pp but preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (CI lower $= 0.610$; Table~\\ref{tab:latex_ablation}).\nOLMo-1B is a capacity failure (0v1 JSD $= 0.33$), not a counter-example; Mistral-7B provides the strongest domain-dependence evidence ($\\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4 but $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv).\nThe arXiv subset ($n{=}2{,}200$ per depth) is smaller than C4 ($n{=}5{,}000$); at equal sample size, wider bootstrap CIs shift borderline C4 configurations below $\\theta$, yielding $\\Kempirical{=}1$; the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ verdict for these marginal C4 cases requires ${\\geq}5{,}000$ samples.\n\n\\paragraph{Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$): single-generation saturation.}\nWith cross-family scorer control, all six models yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 $\\auc$: 0.498--0.595, all below $\\theta$): encyclopedic regularity provides minimal signal beyond the 0v1 boundary.\nSame-family scoring inflates Qwen to $\\Kempirical{=}2$ ($\\Delta = +2.4$\\,pp); cross-family scoring controls this bias (Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}).\n\n\\paragraph{Code ($\\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$): cross-modality confirmation.}\nPython functions from CodeSearchNet~\\cite{husain2019codesearchnet} ($n{=}5{,}000$) yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for four models (1v2 $\\auc$: 0.615--0.739; Table~\\ref{tab:code_crossmodel_kstar}) with the same sharp 2v3 collapse as C4 (JSD ratio $11.7$--$13.5\\times$); Pythia-1.4B is capacity-limited ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, $\\auc = 0.598$; cross-family scorer: $\\Delta \\leq 1.9$\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{sec:code_domain}).\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source generator mixing.}\nMulti-source pooling (three generators per domain; Appendix~\\ref{sec:multi_source_details}) preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains (C4 $\\Kempirical{=}2$, unified 1v2 $\\auc = 0.711$, generator identity $+0.010$ mean $\\auc$ [range: $+0.001$, $+0.022$]; $\\Kempirical$ unchanged in all eight configurations; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$; Code $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Wikipedia $\\Kempirical{=}1$), independently confirming the cross-scorer correction on Wikipedia.\n\n\\noindent\\begin{minipage}{\\columnwidth}\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\captionof{table}{Domain $\\times$ Model $\\Kempirical$ matrix (scorer-controlled). Wikipedia values use cross-family scorer (Pythia-1.4B). $^*$OLMo arXiv: capacity failure. $^\\dagger$Same-family scorer yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$; controlled value shown. $^\\ddagger$Borderline: Pythia CI upper $= 0.603$, Mistral CI lower $= 0.603$.}\n\\label{tab:domain_kstar}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{C4} & \\textbf{Code} & \\textbf{arXiv} & \\textbf{Wikipedia} \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-1.5B    & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1$^\\dagger$ \\\\\nQwen-7B      & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1$^\\dagger$ \\\\\nPythia-1.4B  & 2 & 1$^\\ddagger$ & 3 & 1 \\\\\nOLMo-1B      & 2 & --- & 1$^*$ & 1 \\\\\nGemma-2-2B   & 1 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1 \\\\\nMistral-7B   & 1 & 2$^\\ddagger$ & 3 & 1 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{minipage}\n\n\\paragraph{Summary.}\n\\label{sec:binary_confidence}\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ for web text holds across four of six families (1B--14B), four decoding strategies, and multi-source pools (Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$; binary detectors: $K^*_{\\text{binary}} \\leq 1$, Table~\\ref{tab:s9_binary_spearman}).\nThe domain ordering arXiv $>$ C4 $=$ Code $>$ Wikipedia is $\\theta$-invariant; scale and architecture do not alter this ordering, confirming structural complexity as the primary determinant.\n",
      "numLines": 170,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 170
    }

```text
1	\section{Consistency Check: $\Kempirical$-Guided Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	\begin{table}[t]
5	\centering
6	\caption{Downstream evaluation after fine-tuning Qwen-2.5-1.5B on 30k C4 documents (depths 0--5), 3 seeds. Metrics: PIQA (acc\_norm), TruthfulQA (mc2), WinoGrande (acc), ARC-C (acc\_norm), GSM8K (exact match). Bold: best strategy.}
7	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
8	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}
9	\end{table}
10	
11	The preceding sections establish $\Kempirical{=}2$ as a robust boundary; we test whether this predicts the correct curation strategy by fine-tuning Qwen-2.5-1.5B on 30{,}000 prompt-chain documents (depths 0--5; Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}).
12	Graduated filtering performs indistinguishably from no filtering ($\Delta \leq 0.53$\,pp across five benchmarks), confirming that depth weighting beyond $\Kempirical$ captures no signal; binary filtering selectively preserves mathematical reasoning (GSM8K $+$1.92\,pp) and factual accuracy (TruthfulQA $+$1.52\,pp) but degrades commonsense reasoning (WinoGrande $-$0.79\,pp).
13	All strategies incur capability cost relative to the base model; binary filtering partially mitigates this for reasoning tasks.
14	Effect sizes are small (max $+$1.92\,pp); these results demonstrate strategy ordering rather than absolute improvement.
15	Cross-model replication and rate sweeps (10--100\%) confirm graduated $\approx$ no-filter ordering (Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}, \ref{sec:pythia_filtering}, \ref{sec:rate_sweep}).
16	
```

whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\nDomain determines the $\\Kempirical$ ordering; Wikipedia's template-driven structure causes single-generation saturation ($\\Kempirical{=}1$; Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\nAbsolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv (Tables~\\ref{tab:wiki_deberta_comparison}, \\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), but matches the 15D bound on C4 and Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary} on web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}).\nThe progression 15D $\\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\\text{neural}}{=}3$) $\\to$ DeBERTa ($\\Kempirical{=}4$) shows diminishing returns, suggesting $K^*_\\text{theory} \\approx 3$--$4$; permutation importance confirms saturation at 2v3 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:feature_importance}).\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, partially addressing the 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}): domain-calibrated ceilings hold under mixed-generator training even though $\\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed when the generator is unknown.\n\n",

  "old_string": "We present the first empirical measurement of $\\Kempirical$ across six architecture families, four domains, and four decoding strategies.\nFor web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ holds across four of six families: a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper recursive content.\nThe $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across generators under 15D features; C4/Code $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is representation-independent (confirmed by DeBERTa), while 15D provides a conservative lower bound on structured domains.\nLeave-one-family-out cross-validation confirms that $\\theta{=}0.60$ is robust across held-out families, and a non-Qwen derivation with Qwen validation rules out scorer overfitting.\nFor data curation, binary filtering at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary suffices ($\\Delta \\leq 0.53$\\,pp for graduated vs.\\ no filtering); retrain-chain experiments extend the range to $\\Kempirical \\in [2, 7]$, with scaling beyond 14B and mixed chains remaining open.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops ${\\sim}$30\\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\\theta$).\nThis resolves filtering granularity: graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), and the $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering extends this across domains.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}2$, text-intrinsic information loss).\nRetrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:retrain_chain}), bounding the range; whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$); domain determines the $\\Kempirical$ ordering, with Wikipedia's template-driven structure causing single-generation saturation.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\nAbsolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv but matches 15D on C4 and Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary} on web text.\nThe progression 15D $\\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\\text{neural}}{=}3$) $\\to$ DeBERTa ($K^*{=}4$) suggests $K^*_\\text{theory} \\approx 3$--$4$.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), confirming that ceilings hold under mixed-generator training even when the generator is unknown.\n\n",

    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

### User

_source line 4503 · 2026-05-21T12:18:09.389Z_

[飞书用户 冯元宁 (ou_892b8a1f2776e36eaf541a0c90fc4619)]: 你确认下面这些问题是否存在（中间一些问题可能不存在，如果你认为确实没有问题，就不用改，但是你要告诉我一下你认为哪些部分没有问题，以及为什么）

**1. Figure 4 的 retrain-chain K\* 明显前后不一致。**
正文说 retrain-chain yields `K*=7`，Figure 4 caption 也说 dotted red 是 `K*=7`，但图例里显示的是类似 `Retrain-chain, K*≈4`。这属于非常显眼的低级错误，审稿人一眼就会怀疑图是不是旧版本。正文同页还反复把 retrain-chain 写成 `K*=7`。

**2. Figure 4 的 C4 模型数量也不一致。**
图例写的是 “C4, K*=2 (8 models, min-max)”，caption 写的是 “7 continuation-mode C4 models”。如果 8 是把 rewrite 也算进去了，那 caption 不能写 continuation-mode；如果只算 continuation-mode，那图例不该写 8。这个也很像后期更新图/文字时没同步。

**3. changepoint 结果前后矛盾。**
主文 Section 4.1 写 exact agreement 是 `18/37 (49%)`，并且说有 `19 disagreements`。但附录 D.27 写的是 exact agreement `17/37 (45.9%)`，按这个应该是 20 个 disagreements。这个不是表述问题，是数字互相打架。主文还把摘要/贡献里写成 “changepoint-confirmed”，但主文自己又说 exact agreement 只有约一半，而且 “We do not rely on changepoint agreement”。这会显得 claim 过强。

**4. Table 32 的 Pythia 数字极可疑。**
Table 2 里 Pythia 1v2 AUC 是 `.6539`，Table 31 的 full 15D 是 `0.6262`，但 Table 32 里 Pythia-1.4B suddenly 变成 `.8840`。这个 `.8840` 又和后面 Pythia-greedy 的 `.8838` 非常接近，看起来像把 greedy decoding 的结果误标成了普通 Pythia-1.4B。这个属于必须核对原始实验表的错误。

**5. 多处表格 caption 声称有 95% CI，但表里很多格子没有 CI。**
例如 Table 2 标题写 “with bootstrap 95% CIs”，但 0v1、3v4、4v5 的很多配置只有 AUC，没有 CI；Table 10 也类似。这种不是大科学问题，但非常低级，会被认为 reporting 不完整。

6. Cross-Domain Variation 的配置数量写错。
Section 4.5 写 “across six configurations spanning four families”，但紧接着 Table 4 实际列了 7 个 model rows：Qwen-1.5B、Qwen-7B、Pythia、OLMo、Gemma、LLaMA、Mistral，而且 families 也不是 four。这里至少要改成与 Table 4 一致。
7. Table 10 caption 本身也混乱。
它说 “9 C4 configurations”，又说 “Gemma-2-2B reported in Table 4”；但 Table 10 里实际有 Qwen-rw 这个 rewrite ablation，Gemma 又不在里面。然后 caption 又说 “7/9 continuation-mode configurations”，这会让读者数不清到底 9 是哪些。
8. 若干 cross-reference 指错表。
Page 8 说 domain ordering extends across domains，引用的是 Table 35；但 Table 35 是 DeBERTa vs. OBD on arXiv，不是 domain ordering。Page 8 又说 representation-independent boundary on web text 引 Table 29；但 Table 29 是 DeBERTa vs. OBD filtering，不是最直接的 boundary 证据。Wikipedia template-driven K=1 引 Table 44，而 Table 44 是 lexical statistics，不是 K matrix。
9. “near-chance 5-class accuracy (~0.33)” 表述不太稳。
5-class chance 是 0.20，0.33 不算 near-chance，尤其还和 OBD baseline 0.386 很接近。更稳的写法是 “substantially below OBD / poor depth recovery beyond binary separation”，不要说 near-chance。
10. “all methods fail at depths ≥2” 有点过。
Table 12 里 GPT-5.5 depth-2 recall 是 38%，Claude depth-4 recall 是 37%，虽然确实不强，但直接说 “all methods fail” 容易被挑。应改成 “do not reliably recover ordered depth beyond the boundary”。

```text
990	\bottomrule
991	\end{tabular}
992	\end{table}
993	
994	\subsection{Changepoint $\Kempirical$ Validation}
995	\label{sec:changepoint_validation}
996	
997	To verify that $\Kempirical$ is not an artifact of the fixed threshold $\theta{=}0.60$, we apply non-parametric changepoint detection (PELT with $\ell_2$ cost, penalty $= 0.1$; BinSeg and BottomUp as robustness checks) to the pairwise AUC sequences for all 37 experiment configurations.
998	The changepoint-derived $\Kempirical_{\text{CP}}$ agrees with $\theta{=}0.60$-based $\Kempirical$ within $\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (94.6\%; exact agreement: 17/37, 45.9\%).
999	In all disagreement cases, the changepoint method is more conservative ($\Kempirical_{\text{CP}} \leq \Kempirical_\theta$), with disagreements occurring when boundary AUC values fall in the range $[0.607, 0.654]$.
1000	The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is preserved under the changepoint criterion.
1001	Bootstrap resampling ($n{=}2{,}000$) confirms stability of the changepoint location.
1002	
1003	\subsection{Benjamini-Hochberg Multiple Testing Correction}
1004	\label{sec:bh_correction}
1005	
1006	The method section reports permutation $p \leq 10^{-4}$ for all boundary comparisons (Section~\ref{sec:formulation}).
1007	Benjamini-Hochberg correction across all $m{=}30$ boundary tests (10 C4 configurations $\times$ 3 boundary pairs) yields $q_{\max} = 0.003 \ll 0.05$.
1008	Under an extreme upper bound of $m{=}185$ (all pairwise depth comparisons across all configurations), $q_{\max} = 0.019 < 0.05$.
1009	Multiple testing therefore does not affect any $\Kempirical$ determination.
1010	
1011	\subsection{Feature Importance Analysis}
1012	\label{sec:feature_importance}
1013	
1014	Permutation importance (10 repetitions, mean $\Delta\auc$) on LLaMA-3.1-8B C4 generations (Qwen-1.5B scorer) reveals a qualitative shift in discriminative features across depth boundaries (Figure~\ref{fig:feature_importance}; Table~\ref{tab:perm_importance}).
1015	For 0v1, first-order surprisal derivative standard deviation ($\Delta^1$ Surprisal Std) accounts for 41.7\% of total importance (0.209 of 0.502 summed $\Delta\auc$), reflecting the sharp distributional shift at generation onset.
1016	At the $\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), the importance profile shifts: distributional spread (Surprisal Std, $\Delta\auc{=}0.058$) and second-order derivative statistics ($\Delta^2$ Surprisal Std, 0.022) dominate, while derivative features that drive 0v1 become negligible.
1017	For 2v3, all 15 features show near-random importance ($\Delta\auc \leq 0.01$; total $\auc{=}0.532$), confirming that no individual feature retains discriminative power beyond $\Kempirical{=}2$.
1018	
1019	\begin{figure}[t]

240	
241	To verify that $\Kempirical$ does not depend on the self\_BLEU feature (which may reflect stylistic repetition rather than depth-correlated degradation), we compare 15D and 14D (self\_BLEU removed) classifiers across four model configurations (Table~\ref{tab:selfbleu_ablation}).
242	
243	\begin{table}[htbp]
244	\centering
245	\small
246	\caption{15D vs.\ 14D (self\_BLEU removed) 1v2 $\auc$ with bootstrap 95\% CIs. Removing self\_BLEU does not change $\Kempirical$ for any configuration. Maximum $|\Delta\auc| = 0.0031$, within bootstrap CI width (${\sim}0.018$).}
247	\label{tab:selfbleu_ablation}
248	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
249	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
250	\toprule
251	\textbf{Model} & $\Kempirical$ & \textbf{15D $\auc$ [CI]} & \textbf{14D $\auc$ [CI]} & $\Delta$ \\
252	\midrule
253	Qwen-base   & 2/2 & $.6288$ $[.620,\,.638]$ & $.6258$ $[.616,\,.635]$ & $-.0031$ \\
254	Pythia-1.4B  & 2/2 & $.8840$ $[.878,\,.890]$ & $.8830$ $[.876,\,.889]$ & $-.0010$ \\
255	LLaMA-8B     & 2/2 & $.6075$ $[.598,\,.617]$ & $.6074$ $[.598,\,.617]$ & $-.0001$ \\
256	Mistral-7B   & 1/1 & $.5902$ $[.580,\,.600]$ & $.5903$ $[.580,\,.600]$ & $+.0002$ \\
257	\bottomrule
258	\end{tabular}}
259	\end{table}
260	
261	All four configurations preserve their $\Kempirical$ under 14D, with negligible AUC change ($|\Delta| \leq 0.003$).
262	The self\_BLEU feature contributes minimally to the depth boundary; $\Kempirical{=}2$ is driven by surprisal and entropy features rather than lexical repetition statistics.
263	
264	\subsection{Wikipedia Domain: Scorer Confound Resolution}
265	\label{sec:wiki_domain}
266	
267	Under same-family scoring (Qwen-1.5B scorer), Wikipedia $\Kempirical$ appeared to exhibit model-family dependence: Qwen generators (1.5B and 7B) yielded $\Kempirical{=}2$, while all four non-Qwen models yielded $\Kempirical{=}1$ (Table~\ref{tab:wiki_domain}).
268	A scorer confound ablation resolves this apparent split.
269	Rescoring the same Qwen-7B Wikipedia text with Pythia-1.4B reduces 1v2 $\auc$ from $0.611$ to $0.587$ ($\Delta = -2.4$\,pp), dropping $\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1.
270	With the scorer confound controlled, all six models yield $\Kempirical{=}1$ on Wikipedia, and the domain ordering simplifies to: arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$).
271

276	\label{tab:wiki_domain}
277	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%

    "file": {
      "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/appendix_extended.tex",
      "content": "\nTo verify that $\\Kempirical$ does not depend on the self\\_BLEU feature (which may reflect stylistic repetition rather than depth-correlated degradation), we compare 15D and 14D (self\\_BLEU removed) classifiers across four model configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:selfbleu_ablation}).\n\n\\begin{table}[htbp]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{15D vs.\\ 14D (self\\_BLEU removed) 1v2 $\\auc$ with bootstrap 95\\% CIs. Removing self\\_BLEU does not change $\\Kempirical$ for any configuration. Maximum $|\\Delta\\auc| = 0.0031$, within bootstrap CI width (${\\sim}0.018$).}\n\\label{tab:selfbleu_ablation}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & $\\Kempirical$ & \\textbf{15D $\\auc$ [CI]} & \\textbf{14D $\\auc$ [CI]} & $\\Delta$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-base   & 2/2 & $.6288$ $[.620,\\,.638]$ & $.6258$ $[.616,\\,.635]$ & $-.0031$ \\\\\nPythia-1.4B  & 2/2 & $.8840$ $[.878,\\,.890]$ & $.8830$ $[.876,\\,.889]$ & $-.0010$ \\\\\nLLaMA-8B     & 2/2 & $.6075$ $[.598,\\,.617]$ & $.6074$ $[.598,\\,.617]$ & $-.0001$ \\\\\nMistral-7B   & 1/1 & $.5902$ $[.580,\\,.600]$ & $.5903$ $[.580,\\,.600]$ & $+.0002$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nAll four configurations preserve their $\\Kempirical$ under 14D, with negligible AUC change ($|\\Delta| \\leq 0.003$).\nThe self\\_BLEU feature contributes minimally to the depth boundary; $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is driven by surprisal and entropy features rather than lexical repetition statistics.\n\n\\subsection{Wikipedia Domain: Scorer Confound Resolution}\n\\label{sec:wiki_domain}\n\nUnder same-family scoring (Qwen-1.5B scorer), Wikipedia $\\Kempirical$ appeared to exhibit model-family dependence: Qwen generators (1.5B and 7B) yielded $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while all four non-Qwen models yielded $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Table~\\ref{tab:wiki_domain}).\nA scorer confound ablation resolves this apparent split.\nRescoring the same Qwen-7B Wikipedia text with Pythia-1.4B reduces 1v2 $\\auc$ from $0.611$ to $0.587$ ($\\Delta = -2.4$\\,pp), dropping $\\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1.\nWith the scorer confound controlled, all six models yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$ on Wikipedia, and the domain ordering simplifies to: arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$).\n\n\\begin{table}[htbp]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Wikipedia pairwise $\\auc$ across 6 models. $^\\dagger$Qwen rows use same-family scorer (Qwen-1.5B); cross-family scoring (Pythia-1.4B) reduces Qwen-7B to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ ($\\Delta = -2.4$\\,pp), resolving the apparent family split.}\n\\label{tab:wiki_domain}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{llccccc}\n\\toprule",
      "numLines": 40,
      "startLine": 240,
      "totalLines": 1063
    }
  },

Claude Opus~4.6 shows more uniform errors across depths 1--4,\n    with depth-4 recall (0.3700) substantially exceeding GPT-5.5.\n    Per-depth recall comparison with OBD-LightGBM in Table~\\ref{tab:s3c_perdepth}.}\n  \\label{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}\n\n  \\vspace{0.5em}\n  \\begin{subtable}{0.48\\textwidth}\n    \\centering\n    \\caption{GPT-5.5 ($N{=}500$, acc${=}0.332$, MAE${=}1.102$)}\n    \\label{tab:s3a_gpt55}\n    \\small\n    \\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n      \\toprule\n      & \\multicolumn{5}{c}{Predicted Depth} & \\\\\n      \\cmidrule(lr){2-6}\n      True & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4 & Rec. \\\\\n      \\midrule",

35	
36	% === Abstract ===
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
39	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$, defined as the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain statistically distinguishable, under prompt-chain generation using largely generator-invariant statistical features and an ordinal classifier.
40	Across four domains and six architecture families ($\leq$14B), $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for web text emerges as the dominant mode (four of six families; changepoint-confirmed); among families with complete domain coverage, the ordering arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) holds.
41	$\Kempirical$ values are feature-set dependent: DeBERTa matches the 15D bound on C4 and Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$, a representation-independent boundary) but extends $\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv, placing 15D measurements as conservative lower bounds on structured domains.
42	Multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains with negligible generator-identity signal; features transfer across generators (80--92\% aggregate accuracy, driven by high 0v1 separability), but boundary-level $\Kempirical$ assignments fail under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$).
43	As a consistency check, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.
44	\end{abstract}
45

47	\input{introduction}
48

449	
450	\subsection{JSD Decay Analysis}
451	\label{sec:jsd_extended}
452	
453	The token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two distinct decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}).
454	For C4 web text, 0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$ across all 8 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$ (median cliff ratio ${\sim}$15$\times$), and subsequent pairs converge to a noise floor of ${\sim}$0.003--0.005.
455	Fitting $\jsd(k, k{+}1) \propto e^{-\lambda k}$ yields $\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$ for C4 (mean ${\approx}\,2.9$), consistent with rapid mixing of $G_\delta$ toward its stationary distribution.
456	These decay rates represent qualitative characterizations from 5-point curves rather than precise estimates.
457	The empirical decay rate $\lambda$ is consistent within each domain but varies across domains: C4 yields $\lambda \approx 2.9$, Wikipedia $\lambda \approx 3.1$ (excluding OLMo, whose $\lambda = 6.3$ reflects a capacity-limited collapse: 0v1 $\jsd = 0.169$ with near-complete information loss at the first step, the exponential fit is therefore unreliable), and arXiv $\lambda \approx 1.0$.
458	This cross-model consistency indicates that the contraction rate of $G_\delta$ is governed primarily by the structure of the input domain rather than the model architecture.
459	ArXiv text decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower than C4 ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$), with a correspondingly higher convergence floor (${\sim}$0.007--0.009 vs.\ ${\sim}$0.003--0.004).
460	Formulaic structural features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns, section templates) degrade more slowly under autoregressive generation and supply persistent depth-distinguishing signal, this accounts for the elevated $\Kempirical{=}3$.
461	The retrain-chain exhibits a fundamentally different profile: $\lambda = 0.285$ with a cliff ratio of only 1.31$\times$ and no sharp drop between adjacent pairs (Figure~\ref{fig:retrain_decay}).
462	This gradual, sustained divergence reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts rather than text-intrinsic information loss, further supporting the distinction between prompt-chain and retrain-chain $\Kempirical$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:boundary_extended}).
463	
464	\begin{figure}[htbp]

_source line 4528 · 2026-05-21T12:19:15.728Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01V1jXCnxscQYJi26Wvs94s2`</summary>

```text
25	We present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) and measures it under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.
26	We operationalize $\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \geq 1$ where the pairwise $\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\ref{sec:formulation}).
27	
28	Our contributions:
29	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
30	  \item \textbf{First empirical measurement of $\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\theta{=}0.60$: four of six families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ on C4 ($p \leq 10^{-4}$; changepoint-confirmed; margin stratification in Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains ($+0.010$ mean $\auc$ for generator identity across four domains).
31	  Zero-shot frontier LLMs likewise collapse beyond $\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at depths $\geq 2$ ranges from 8--38\% (vs.\ 20\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Section~\ref{sec:why_kstar}).
32	  \item \textbf{Generator-invariant domain hierarchy and representation-independent boundary}: arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across tested generators under 15D statistics; C4 and Code $\Kempirical{=}2$ is representation-independent (confirmed by DeBERTa fine-tuning), while structured domains show feature-dependent $\Kempirical$ with 15D as a conservative lower bound (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}).
33	  \item \textbf{Transfer and consistency check}: largely generator-invariant features within continuation mode enable 80--92\% accuracy retention among tested configurations ($\rho > 0.90$; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}), though boundary-level $\Kempirical$ assignments are fragile under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$); as a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\Kempirical$ correctly predicts binary ${>}$ graduated filtering for web text (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
34	\end{enumerate}
35	
```

      "content": "We present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) and measures it under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{First empirical measurement of $\\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\\theta{=}0.60$: four of six families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ on C4 ($p \\leq 10^{-4}$; 17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops ${\sim}$30\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\theta$).
19	This resolves filtering granularity: graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}), and the $\theta$-invariant domain ordering extends this across domains.
20	
21	\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain.}
22	All main results use prompt-chain ($\Kempirical{=}2$, text-intrinsic information loss).
23	Retrain-chain yields $\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:retrain_chain}), bounding the range.
24	
25	\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}
26	Token-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$), and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$); domain determines the $\Kempirical$ ordering, with Wikipedia's template-driven structure causing single-generation saturation.
27	
28	\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\Kempirical_{\text{15D}}$ vs.\ $\Kempirical_{\text{intrinsic}}$.}
29	Absolute $\Kempirical$ values are \emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv but matches 15D on C4 and Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \emph{representation-independent boundary} on web text.
30	The progression 15D $\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\text{neural}}{=}3$) $\to$ DeBERTa ($K^*{=}4$) suggests $K^*_\text{theory} \approx 3$--$4$.
31	
32	\paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}
33	Multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}), confirming that ceilings hold under mixed-generator training even when the generator is unknown.

35

domain determines the $\\Kempirical$ ordering, with Wikipedia's template-driven structure causing single-generation saturation.\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\nAbsolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+2$ on Wikipedia and $+1$ on arXiv but matches 15D on C4 and Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary} on web text.\nThe progression 15D $\\to$ neural probes ($K^*_{\\text{neural}}{=}3$) $\\to$ DeBERTa ($K^*{=}4$) suggests $K^*_\\text{theory} \\approx 3$--$4$.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source pooling and transfer.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), confirming that ceilings hold under mixed-generator training even when the generator is unknown.\n\n",

changepoint-confirmed); among families with complete domain coverage, the ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds.\n$\\Kempirical$ values are feature-set dependent: DeBERTa matches the 15D bound on C4 and Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$, a representation-independent boundary) but extends $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv, placing 15D measurements as conservative lower bounds on structured domains.\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains with negligible generator-identity signal; features transfer across generators (80--92\\% aggregate accuracy, driven by high 0v1 separability), but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments fail under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$).\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nAll experiments are conducted at $\\leq$14B scale (6 architecture families, 10 configurations) with English text across four domains and four decoding strategies; whether $\\Kempirical{=}2$ holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.\nMain results use prompt-chain only, isolating text-intrinsic degradation; retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:retrain_chain}), and mixed-chain scenarios are not addressed.\nCross-model transfer retains 80--92\\% aggregate accuracy but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments remain fragile (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\nThe 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound; DeBERTa extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+1$--$2$ on structured domains, suggesting headroom remains.\n$\\Kempirical$ is $\\theta$-dependent (ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$), though a $\\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations.\nThe filtering evaluation validates on two architectures with small effect sizes (max $+$1.92\\,pp), demonstrating strategy ordering rather than large absolute gains.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",
