    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}.\nWhen synthetic text is recursively fed back, multi-generational content degrades with each iteration~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model} through two distinct mechanisms: \\emph{retrain-chains} accumulate degradation in model weights through iterative self-training, while \\emph{prompt-chains} degrade text statistics through recursive generation by a fixed model.\nWe study prompt-chains, which isolate text-intrinsic information loss and model the dominant web contamination pathway, where synthetic text is produced by one-shot LLM generation rather than iterative retraining.\nRetrain-chain experiments place the discrimination boundary higher ($\\Kempirical{=}7$; Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}), framing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor within the $[2,7]$ range.\nFor practitioners curating web corpora, the key question is not whether degradation occurs, but at what generational depth it ceases to be detectable.\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically and ignores the generational depth dimension.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nThis gap cuts across multiple communities: binary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}), membership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and data selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method provides a per-text operational estimate of generational depth backed by statistical guarantees.\n\nFor data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth~2, binary filtering becomes the principled choice; existing model collapse analyses are population-level and do not localize this boundary per text.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality implies monotonic cumulative information loss across generations; together with Markov chain convergence (adjacent transitions $Y_{k-1} \\to Y_k$ become increasingly similar as the chain approaches stationarity), this motivates a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$, whose actual value must be determined empirically.\nWe deliberately employ simple, interpretable features to establish a conservative lower bound on the discrimination boundary; DeBERTa fine-tuning (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}) confirms this design by pushing $\\Kempirical$ higher on structured domains while matching the 15D bound on web text.\nWe present an empirical characterization study that formalizes $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) and measures it under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{First empirical measurement of $\\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\\theta{=}0.60$: four of six families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ on C4 ($p \\leq 10^{-4}$; changepoint-confirmed; margin stratification in Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains ($+0.010$ mean $\\auc$ for generator identity across four domains).\n  Zero-shot frontier LLMs likewise collapse beyond $\\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at depths $\\geq 2$ ranges from 8--38\\% (vs.\\ 20\\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Generator-invariant domain hierarchy and representation-independent boundary}: arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across tested generators under 15D statistics; C4 and Code $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is representation-independent (confirmed by DeBERTa fine-tuning), while structured domains show feature-dependent $\\Kempirical$ with 15D as a conservative lower bound (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Transfer and consistency check}: largely generator-invariant features within continuation mode enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention among tested configurations ($\\rho > 0.90$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), though boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments are fragile under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$); as a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts binary ${>}$ graduated filtering for web text (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
